### On the Spirit of Wine, Chapter IX.

Above, where I mentioned the **essences** of herbs, I showed that they are nothing other than wine; you ought to understand it in this way. The **spirit of wine** <note>original: "spiritus vini"; high-purity alcohol obtained through repeated distillation</note> comes forth from the substance of the wine itself: for wine is the underlying subject of this, just as **horehound** <note>original: "marrubium"; a bitter medicinal herb</note> is the subject of its own native wine. Therefore, so that you may have the spirit of wine as an essence—which is truly an **elixir**—take it thus: Just as one pound of **smartweed** <note>original: "persicariae"; a plant often used in alchemy for its perceived sharp or "fiery" qualities</note> gives off 2 **scruples** <note>a scruple is an apothecary weight of about 1.3 grams</note> of wine, so one pound of wine does not contain more than 1 scruple [of spirit]. The rest is the **phlegm** <term>phlegm; the watery, tasteless, and medically inactive part of a substance that remains after the "spirit" has been removed</term> of the wine, which contributes little to the present elixir. 

Furthermore, the investigation of this essence should be done in this manner: **Digest** <term>digest; a process of applying gentle, consistent heat to a substance over a long period</term> the wine poured into a **pelican** <note>a "pelican" was a specialized circulatory distillation vessel with two side arms that fed condensed vapors back into the main body, shaped like the mythical bird pecking its own breast</note> in **horse dung** <note>original: "fimo equino"; a common method for providing steady, low-level heat in a laboratory before the invention of modern heaters</note>; for a continuous space of two months, you will see it become so thin and pure that it spontaneously draws out a certain **fatness** <note>likely referring to the oily, concentrated ethanol or essential oils</note> on the surface, which is the spirit of wine. Furthermore, whatever is beneath it is phlegm, existing without any of the nature of wine. The fatness, how-

### The Extraction of Mummy, Chapter X.

The extraction of the virtues from **mummy** <term>mummy; "mummia" was a cornerstone of Renaissance medicine, believed to be the "microcosmic" essence of life preserved in the body, often sourced from bitumen or ancient remains</term> is performed in a masterly way—to use that expression—by mixing it with the **essence of wine** <note>alcohol</note> derived from **celandine** <note>original: "chelidonia"; a yellow-flowered herb traditionally used for liver and eye disorders</note>. Specifically, you should digest it for up to ten days and distill it in five. Furthermore, let it be digested again from the start until the mummy turns itself into a liquid. Once this is done, and the upper and lower parts are separated from the middle, add to this a sixteenth part of wood-balsam, a twelfth part by weight of **sealed earth** <term>sealed earth; "terra sigillata," a specialized medicinal clay from the island of Lemnos, traditionally stamped with a seal to prove its purity against poisons</term>, and an equal amount of the **liquor of the horizon** <note>original: "horizontis liquo[rem]"; an alchemical term likely referring to a purified distillate or a specific state of a solvent</note>...

<summary>This page describes how to enhance a life-prolonging elixir with gold and spices to treat physical tension, and begins a technical guide on extracting the medicinal essence of "mummy" using spirits of celandine.</summary>

### *The Extraction of Satyrion, Chapter XI.*

Whatever is to be drawn out from **satyrion** <term>satyrion; a name historically given to various orchids (such as *Orchis mascula*), prized in ancient and Renaissance medicine as a powerful aphrodisiac due to the shape of its roots</term>, must necessarily be done through **separation** <note>the alchemical process of isolating the "pure" medicinal part from the "impure" physical matter</note>. For a **Saturnine** <term>Saturnine; in alchemy, this refers to properties associated with the planet Saturn—cold, heavy, sluggish, or leaden</term> force lies hidden in satyrion, which secretly steals and weakens the very virtue for which the satyrion ought to be praised. Because of this, its **exaltation** <note>exaltatio; a process by which a substance's medicinal power is raised to a higher level</note> is reduced by thirty grains. As a result, it often happens that satyrion, when used, denies the help expected of it. Therefore, in its separation, it is worth considering that it is not the outward form, but only the **virtue** <note>the essential healing power or "soul" of the plant</note> that is separated. This is done according to the following method:

Let the satyrion be **digested** in rye bread, and let this be placed in a **horse's belly** <term>horse's belly; "ventre equino" refers to a pile of fermenting horse manure, which alchemists used as a natural incubator to provide a constant, gentle, and moist heat</term> for the duration of a month. When this time has passed, take the substance itself out of the bread, casting away the dregs. Next, let the "blood" of the satyrion be properly digested and skimmed until it is finished. When it has settled, then at last you have obtained the medicine which, above all others...

<meta>catchword: ...others (original: "ras," likely the ending of "caeteras")</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the preparation of a remedy against poisons and introduces the alchemical extraction of satyrion, emphasizing the need to remove "Saturnine" impurities to unlock its potency.</summary>

### CHAPTER I.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut drop-cap initial 'S' containing two figures, one seated and one standing, set against a background of dense foliage and cross-hatching.</image-desc>**But** lest anything regarding long life be passed over in silence, it is fitting to observe in this little book that within the shell <note>original: "testam"; referring to the outer vessel or the physical form of a substance</note>, even beyond that **fifth entity** <note>original: "quintum illud"; the Quintessence or the highest purified state of a matter</note>, something is enclosed. From this, a certain conjunction—both of the physical and of that which is beyond the body—extends the body into a long life, even beyond that fifth essence. Concerning which, hold this: it is clearly nothing, and invisible; yet in the body itself it is exceptional, providing long life not only to the **microcosmic body** <term>the human body, viewed as a "small world" that mirrors the laws of the entire universe</term>, but it also preserves the **Dardo** <note>likely referring to a specific distilled plant essence or "spear-like" growth</note> whole even until its thirtieth year; it also guards the **Anthos** <note>the flower or volatile essence of rosemary</note> and that noble **Cheyri** <note>the Wallflower (*Cheiranthus cheiri*), valued in alchemy for its restorative properties</note> until the third age. This substance rightly distinguishes itself from the physical balsam of the body; it is a balsam of such a kind that it even excels nature itself, yet it performs its work through a physical operation.

### CHAPTER II.

Furthermore, the explanation of this balsam—which we have just spoken of and which is meant to provide a long life—occurs in two ways. One way is when such a virtue follows secretly and by chance; from this, it follows that a long life is granted to many who, nevertheless, do not in the least recognize the balsam for what it is in itself. The other way is achieved through the **arts** <note>referring to the "spagyric" or alchemical arts of medicine</note>, namely for those who are able to attain that conjunction; yet even so, this cannot be done

### CHAPTER III.

Understand this **Iliaster** <term>the life-sustaining force or "star-material" mentioned above</term> in such a manner, since here three-fold virtues are found beyond the **quintessence** <term>the "fifth element" or the purest, most concentrated essence of a substance</term>. For there is the "made" Iliaster <note>original: "iliafter factitus"; likely referring to something prepared through human art or alchemy</note>, another is the "prepared" Iliaster <note>original: "iliafter paratetus"; perhaps referring to a natural substance made ready for use</note>, and the third is that "great" Iliaster.

Concerning the first, understand it thus: that the "made" Iliaster provides long life according to the diligence of the one who uses it. But the second [the prepared] dispenses that same thing according to favor <note>original: "fauorem"; suggesting either divine grace or a natural predisposition</note>. But the third stands uninjured and without harm in a long life.

Therefore, consider the Iliaster understood in this place: that the first, second, and third are all simultaneously subject to the **microcosm** <term>the "little world," referring to the human being as a reflection of the entire universe</term>, so that one might be able to reduce them into a single **gamonynum** <term>a Paracelsian term derived from the Greek "gamos" (marriage), referring to a chemical or spiritual union of substances</term>. The other is by no means compelled, but just as fa-

For death, time, and choice belong to that third **Iliaster**; it is this force that grants to a head of grain more than nature alone could provide, and even gives fragrance to myrrh and strength to the lilies <note>original: "leris"; likely a variation of "liliis," referring to the strength found in delicate flowers</note>.

But however these things may be, the physician must consider how a connection of this kind might occur in a similar way within the body. Namely, just as the "tree of the sea" <note>likely referring to coral, which was believed to be a plant that hardened into a stone-like state</note>, once fixed, is made independent by that force, and the flow of the **cheyri** <term>an alchemical balsam or the "essence of the stars" believed to preserve health</term> is proven and constant: so too can the same be achieved in the **microcosm** <term>the human body, which mirrors the structures of the great universe</term> through that same connection.

## CHAPTER V.

But regarding that true **Iliaster** <term>the spiritual life-force or "star-born" essence</term>, the matter is such that a thing of this kind can in no way happen without either a sign or a certain necessity directed toward that **Greater Iliaster** <note>The "Greater Iliaster" refers to the vast, cosmic life force of the universe, whereas the "Lesser Iliaster" resides within the individual human.</note>. Understand this point in this sense: that the Iliaster which is about to produce such a long life can by no means perform its work in a place where there is no "room" for that long life. Therefore, observe that such a thing does not happen except through a **transmutation** <term>original: "transmutatione"; a fundamental change in nature or substance</term> of both the location and the elements. It happens in this way: just as the four elements in the shell <note>original: "testa"; here used metaphorically for the human body or the "husk" of the microcosm</note> are mortal, every moment producing a new **generation** <term>original: "generationem"; the process of biological growth and renewal</term>, they also subject it to death...

<sig>i 4</sig>

### CHAPTER VI.

In order that we may further describe what is most necessary in this process regarding the **Iliaster** <term>original: "iliastrum"; the primeval matter of life or the "star of the matter," which Paracelsus believed governed health and longevity</term>: Firstly, so that the impure **animate being** <term>original: "animatum"; the living, ensouled aspect of the person</term> may be purified without the separation of the elements, which is achieved through your own **imagination** <note>original: "imaginationem"; in this context, it refers not to fantasy, but to a focused, creative power of the mind capable of affecting the body's internal chemistry</note>, when it stands firm within the **firmament of your mind** <note>original: "animi tui confirmamento"; the stable, foundational structure of the spirit</note>, beyond all bodily and mechanical labor. 

This disposition indeed arises from this: that a man advances himself in his mind so that he is made equal to the **Enochians** <term>original: "enochdianis"; referring to those who, like the biblical Enoch, have attained a state of spiritual translation or extraordinary longevity</term>. It is not necessarily that he desires to lead an Enochian life, even though he may be diametrically distant from it in his current spirit. 

Therefore, it is necessary for the **microcosm** <note>The human being as a "little world" reflecting the universe</note> in its interior anatomy to be **reverberated** <term>original: "reverberari"; an alchemical process of heating through reflected light or flame, here applied to a spiritual purification</term> even unto the highest reverberation. For through this reverberation, the impure consumes itself; however, the **fixed** part <note>The stable, immortal essence</note>, which is usually destroyed by the impure when they are mixed, remains without **rust** <note>original: "ferrugine"; dross or corruption</note>. 

Yet this is not a fire in which a **salamandrine essence** <note>original: "salamandrina"; fire-resistant or born of fire</note> or a **Melusinian Ares** <term>original: "Melosinicùmue Ares"; Ares is the inner formative power or "craftsman" of the body; Melusina refers to a mercurial or water-spirit, suggesting a balance of fire and moisture</term> could be present, but rather a kind of **retorted distillation** <term>original: "retorta... distillatio"; a process of refinement where the essence is drawn out and condensed</term> from the middle of the center, rising above all fires of coals. Moreover, such a reverberation having been made—

<summary>This page outlines the spiritual alchemy required to purify the human "Iliaster," emphasizing that internal refinement is achieved through the power of the imagination rather than physical labor. It describes a process of "internal reverberation" that removes corruption to reveal the fixed, enduring essence of the microcosm.</summary>

<keywords>Iliaster, imagination, Enochians, microcosm, reverberation, Ares, distillation, purification, Paracelsian alchemy</keywords>

...they exhibit. Thus, from another region, there is also something **fixed in its own firmament** <note>In Paracelsian philosophy, the "firmament" is not just the sky, but the stable, governing structure within the human body that mirrors the stars</note>, and so it remains unshaken and enduring, causing neither disease nor death. Regarding this matter, one may philosophize in this way: that those **fixed spirits** <term>original: "spiritus illi fixi"; stable, non-volatile spiritual forces that provide consistency to life</term> allow nothing to perish entirely; their long life is immobile and firm until their **transmutation** <note>The process of changing from one state of being to another</note>, and then back again into their first state. 

Indeed, a comparison is drawn from the text: that according to the **fixation** (so to speak) of the firmament, long life is more extended in one **strengthening** <term>original: "confirmamento"; refers to the internal support or structural integrity of the spirit</term> than in another; even though all things relate only to the first limit. 

However, certain **inhabitants** <note>original: "incolæ"; Paracelsus often refers to the forces or spiritual entities residing within specific organs or elements as "inhabitants"</note> (for so I choose to call them) also accompany a transmutation of this kind, even unto the tenth or twelfth degree. Their death will follow upon the destruction of that **great firmament**, when both bodies—the celestial as well as the terrestrial—shall be moved, and even the **super-celestial** <note>Refers to the highest spiritual realms beyond the physical stars</note> as well. 

Although this happens without any distinction of nature. For in the first minute, when such changes occur, the **putrefaction** <term>original: "putrefactio"; in alchemy, this is the essential first stage of transformation where a substance breaks down so it can be reborn in a purified state</term> of nature begins, and it happens while the proper body is still surviving and living.

### CHAPTER VI.

Furthermore, in order that we may describe that which is most necessary in this process regarding the **Iliaster** <term>original: "iliastrum"; in Paracelsian thought, this is the primeval matter or the fundamental life-force from which all things grow</term>: First, the impure living thing must be purified without the separation of the elements. This is achieved through your own **imagination**, when it remains fixed in the **strengthening** <term>original: "confirmamento"; the internal spiritual structure or firmament</term> of your mind, far beyond any physical or mechanical labor. 

Indeed, such a state is achieved because a man advances himself in his mind so that he is made equal to the **Enochdians** <note>original: "enochdianis"; referring to those who, like the biblical figure Enoch, have achieved a state of spiritual perfection or extreme longevity that bypasses natural decay</note>. This is not because he desires to lead the life of an Enochian, yet in his mind he spans the distance toward it. For this reason, the **microcosm** <note>The human being viewed as a miniature version of the entire universe</note> must be **reverberated** <term>original: "reuerberari"; an alchemical term for reflecting heat or spiritual energy back onto a substance to purify it</term> within its own internal anatomy, even unto the highest degree of reverberation. 

For through this reverberation, the impure part consumes itself; however, the **fixed** part, which is freed from the impure, remains without **rust** <note>original: "ferrugine"; used here to mean corruption, decay, or spiritual tarnish</note>. Yet this is not a fire in which a **salamandrine essence** <note>original: "salamandrina... essentia"; referring to the elemental spirits of fire</note> or a **Melusinic Ares** <note>original: "Melosinicùmue Ares"; Melusines are spirits of water, and "Ares" is the Paracelsian term for the dispenser of form in nature. Together, they represent the elemental forces of growth</note> could be present. Rather, it is a certain **retorted distillation** <note>A metaphor comparing mental refinement to the process of distilling liquids in a glass retort</note> from the very center, far above all fires of burning coals. When such a reverberation has been performed...

<summary>This page describes a process of spiritual alchemy where the "Iliaster" (life-force) is purified through the power of imagination and internal "reverberation." This process allows a person to achieve a state of mental purity and stability comparable to the long-lived biblical figure Enoch, without physical labor.</summary>

...by this method, in its final limit, it exhibits a physical **lightning** <note>original: "fulmen"; Paracelsus often used meteorological phenomena to describe sudden, powerful energetic shifts or "flashes" of purification within the human body</note>, just as the lightning of **Saturn** and the lightning of the **Sun** separate from each other. Therefore, whatever proceeds through this lightning belongs to long life—specifically, the long life of that great **Iliaster** <term>original: "iliastri"; the fundamental, primeval life-force or "star-matter" within man</term>. And indeed, this lightning-flash and the **reverberation** <term>original: "reuerberatio"; an alchemical term for reflecting heat or spiritual energy back onto a substance to intensify its purity</term> that precedes it do not take anything away from the physical weight, but rather remove the **turbulence** <note>original: "turbulentiæ"; the chaotic or disordered state of the bodily elements</note> of the body, and this occurs according to the proportion of transparent colors.

### CHAPTER VII.

Furthermore, long life is not of this kind [merely] from the **Iliaster** of the first power. For that provides only a lower degree of life. Yet, it is that which performs the separation in that place; and just as a **fixed** <note>stable, purified, and permanent</note> thing can preserve a thing that is **not fixed** <note>volatile, unstable, or prone to decay</note>, it defends the **microcosm** <term>original: "microcoſmum"; the human being seen as a miniature map of the entire universe</term> from death. 

Indeed, its function is not merely to separate, but rather to overcome the **undigested** <term>original: "indigeſtum"; crude, unrefined, or "raw" matter in the body that has not been properly transformed by the internal "alchemist" (the Archeus), leading to disease</term> through those transparent secrets, so that its destruction does not follow. This is similar to one who overcomes evil with good. It is like the **mumia** <term>original: "mumia"; the innate, vital "balsam" or healing power found in living things that prevents putrefaction</term>, which emerges together with the body from birth; the mumia itself is good, while the body is [inherently] flawed and prone to rot. Therefore, whatever life the body lives, it lives from the **mumia** alone...

...mummy <note>original: "mummia"; in Paracelsian medicine, this is not an Egyptian corpse, but the "vital balsam" or innate life-force that prevents the living body from rotting</note>. For its natural and proper tendency is to rot and return again into the **dung** <note>waste or base matter</note> of which it is a part—this is its constant striving. However, the mummy acts differently: it breaks that innate corruption and preserves the body by its own power, preventing the body from eventually accomplishing what it attempts [to decay].

Therefore, the received wisdom states that the **super-celestial mummy** sustains the **microcosm** <term>original: "microcoſmũ"; the human being seen as a miniature map of the entire universe</term> more than the body's own innate mummy does. For as often as there is a mummy, there is likewise another limit; yet neither time nor number is found within such limits. For it endures until it can no longer avoid a **second generation** <note>a transformation or a new state of being, here referring to the transition from healthy tissue to decay</note>.

A physician must be skilled in this principle: that any **first matter** <term>original: "prima materia"; the fundamental, unformed essence of a substance</term> eventually expels its **ultimate matter** <note>the final, completed form or the residue left at the end of a natural process</note>. Consequently, the **generation of worms** <note>historically, it was believed that parasites or "worms" were spontaneously generated by the body as it began to decay</note> begins at the point where the ultimate matter of the physical body reveals itself. Observe this **Iliaster** <term>original: "iliaftrum"; the fundamental life-force or "star-matter" within man</term> then: it not only does not destroy the generation of worms, but even when their matter is present, it does not even hinder their growth—even though the mummy is supposed to prevent it.

### Chapter VII.

Furthermore, from the **Iliaster** of the first power, long life of this kind is not obtained. For it provides a lower degree. But it is nevertheless that which separates in that place, and just as a **fixed thing** <note>in alchemy, a "fixed" substance is stable and resistant to fire/change</note> can preserve a thing that is not fixed, it defends the **microcosm** <term>the human being as a "small world" mirroring the macrocosm of the universe</term> from death. 

Since its operation is not to separate, but rather—through those transparent secrets—to overcome the **undigested** <note>original: "indigestum"; raw, chaotic, or unrefined matter within the body that leads to decay</note>, so that its destruction does not follow. It is like one who overcomes evil with good. Just like the **mummy** <note>original: "mummia"; the internal life-force or vital balsam</note>, which proceeds from birth along with the body; the mummy itself is good, but the body is evil and prone to rot. Therefore, whatever life the body lives, it lives from the mummy alone...

...[mum]mia <note>original: "mia"; continuing the word "mummia" from the previous page</note>: for it is the property and natural state of the body to rot, and return again into dung, of which it is a part; this is its constant endeavor. ### Chapter VIII.

IN conclusion to all these matters, first note that **natural mummy** <term>original: "mummia"; the vital healing balm inherent in all living things that resists decay</term> must be composed of those three **anatomies** <note>In Paracelsian thought, "anatomy" refers to the structural and chemical makeup of a thing across the three principles—salt, sulfur, and mercury—rather than just physical dissection</note>, by which the wandering **microcosm** <term>the human being, viewed as a "small world" mirroring the universe</term> may govern the physical body. This is something that must happen from the outside, whether through the element of water—such as through its metals, salts, and similar substances—or through the element of earth—as through its herbs or mushrooms <note>original: "boletos"</note>—or in **manna** <term>original: "thereniabin"; a term for a sugary dew or celestial exudation believed to have medicinal properties</term>, or <gloss>a</gloss> **nostoch** <term>a type of algae or cyanobacteria that appears after rain, believed by Paracelsians to be "star-jelly" or celestial matter fallen to earth</term>. For all these are the dwelling places of super-celestial things.

Therefore, let no one wonder that the immense virtues of **lemon balm** <note>original: "melissæ"; the herb Melissa officinalis, highly prized in alchemy for its regenerative and life-extending properties</note> are described everywhere. For when a super-celestial conjunction unites itself within this herb, who could deny it a power, however extraordinary? And these are indeed the **great wonders** <note>original: "magnalia"; the hidden, powerful works of God within nature</note> which **Bamahemi** <term>a Paracelsian term for a specific spiritual or balsamic virtue found in nature</term> also contains within itself; and it is clearly the **iloch** <term>the fundamental creative or formative power in nature</term> which, being composed of the true **aniado** <term>the vital, astral power that governs life, health, and the fruits of the earth</term>, can in no way be taken away from that which is composed of the elements. 

This occurs through the **exaltations** <term>alchemical processes that raise a substance to a higher state of purity, intensity, or power</term> of both worlds: just as the exaltations of the **nettle** <note>original: "urticæ"</note> cause it to burn, and the color of a small flame <note>original: "flammulæ"</note> shines and glows. In exaltations of this kind, however, their virtue [is able] to be transferred into another...

<meta>catchword: po- (short for "poterit" or "potest," meaning "can" or "is able")</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that the human body's internal vital force (mummy) is sustained by celestial virtues found in specific plants, minerals, and substances like nostoch. He emphasizes that these "exaltations" represent the spiritual powers of nature being concentrated into physical forms for healing.</summary>

...is possible. Therefore, learn to recognize the **exaltations** <term>original: "exaltationes"; alchemical processes where a substance is raised to its highest potency or "exalted" state</term> in this manner; these are far more powerful than nettles <gloss>a</gloss> <note>original: "urtica"; likely used here as a metaphor for common, stinging, or crude medicinal plants</note>, and you should collect them just as in the true **May** <note>The "philosophical May" refers to the ideal time in spring to collect dew and celestial influences when nature is at its peak vitality</note>, when the exaltations of the **Aniadora** <term>original: "Aniadorum"; the vital, life-giving spiritual powers of the elements and stars</term> begin. For the exaltations of these virtues are not located only in the **matrices** <gloss>b</gloss> <term>original: "matricibus"; the "wombs" of nature, specifically the four elements (Earth, Water, Air, Fire)</term>, but also in the super-celestial realms. Indeed, that **Idæus** <term>original: "Idæus"; referring to the "Archeus" or the internal master craftsman of nature who shapes matter</term> would be common and of no value if he only knew how to manufacture a single thing subject to the eyes, and had not created certain greater things beyond those that can be touched. 

In truth, he has constructed a different kind of "May," where super-celestial flowers exalt themselves. In this "May," the **Anachmus** <term>original: "Anachmus"; the incorporeal essence or spiritual breath of life that sustains the body's longevity</term> must be extracted and preserved, no differently than musk is kept in a **pomander** <note>original: "pomambra"; a perforated container for perfumes or aromatics, often worn to protect against pestilence</note>, and just as the virtue of gold lies hidden in **laudanum** <term>original: "Laudano"; in Paracelsian medicine, a "most praiseworthy" remedy of high potency, often containing gold or specific resins, distinct from the later opium tincture</term>. When the virtues of this kind of Anachmus are collected, then at last you may deservedly rejoice in a **long life**.

## Book Five.

### Chapter I.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial letter 'S' featuring two figures, likely cherubs or spirits, positioned within the curves of the ornamental letter.</image-desc>

In the first book above, I laid the foundations of long life; here, we shall bring them to completion. Indeed, I see no reason to repeat here those matters which pertain to a full and healthy life, since enough has been said about them by us in the previous books, the second and third. Therefore, in this fifth book, I shall put the finishing touches <note>original: "supremam manum imponam"; a Latin idiom meaning to complete or perfect a work</note> on those things we began in the first book regarding the life that exists **beyond nature** <note>original: "præter naturam"; referring to a life span extended beyond the ordinary biological limit through alchemical or spiritual means</note>; and I shall do so according to theoretical reasoning.

First, we propose to all **spagyrics** <term>original: "spagyricis"; alchemists who practice the art of separating and recombining substances to create medicines</term> the age of both **Adam** and **Methuselah** <note>According to the Bible, Adam lived 930 years and Methuselah 969 years; Paracelsus viewed these ages as the potential limit for human life through alchemical perfection</note>, which will be detailed in the second chapter that follows. However, before we arrive at those points, we shall first speak precisely of the long life which is in the hand of that greatest **iliaster** <term>original: "iliastis"; in Paracelsian philosophy, the Iliaster is the primordial, vital force of the universe or the spiritual seed of matter that grants longevity</term>, and we shall do so according to the principle of the **magnalia** <term>original: "magnalium"; the "great works" or hidden miracles of God found within the secrets of nature</term>. In this matter, I advise that much must be understood concerning **free will**,

<meta>catchword: than</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus introduces the final book of his treatise, stating his intent to explain how human life can be extended to the biblical ages of Adam and Methuselah by accessing the "iliaster" or primordial vital force. He emphasizes that this knowledge completes his earlier teachings on lives extended beyond natural biological limits.</summary>

<keywords>Paracelsus, long life, Methuselah, Adam, spagyrics, iliaster, magnalia, free will, alchemy, longevity</keywords>

--- Page 201 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of Book Five, Chapter I, moving from the historical examples of long-lived patriarchs like Adam to the metaphysical mechanisms—specifically the "Enochdian" influence and the "iliaster"—that allow for life to be sustained beyond the limits of the four elements.</meta>

<page-num>87</page-num>
<page-type>text</page-type>
<header>LONG LIFE, BOOK V.</header>

than those things which we are able to manage from the elements. To make these matters clear and more easily understood <note>original: "clarius ac rudius"; literally "more clearly and more crudely," meaning simplified for the general reader</note>, we must first imagine the **Enochdians** <term>original: "Enochdanos"; a state of being or influence named after the biblical Enoch, who was taken by God without dying. In this context, it refers to a life-force exempt from natural decay</term>. If we carry this influence within our mind—just as fire is carried within wood—and not without a common power, it is possible for us to reach this life with no effort at all. This is made evident by the example of **John** <note>original: "Ioharnetis"; likely referring to John the Apostle, who in medieval legend was said to remain alive, or merely "sleeping," until the Second Coming</note>, whose nature encompassed not just a single lifetime, nor merely a single century.

But lest in these my **Paramiric** <term>original: "paramiricis"; referring to Paracelsus’s "Paramirum," a series of works investigating the spiritual and invisible causes of disease</term> books I provide an opportunity for slander to those who twist the scriptures in various ways, we shall define nothing for certain in this first chapter concerning the life of the supreme **iliaster** <term>original: "iliastis"; the primordial vital force or the spiritual essence from which life is sustained</term>: whether he exists here among the physical elements, or whether he lives further on in the **fifth essence** <note>original: "in quinto"; the quintessence, a spiritual or celestial substance beyond the four physical elements of earth, water, air, and fire</note> where no physical body is found—the place where not only those we have mentioned live, but also those whom we believe to be buried in sleep.

All these things, I say, we leave for the higher spirits to contemplate for the time being, until we come to those matters later on. Furthermore, if that supreme **iliaster** were to be impelled, or if he at least had need of something, whatever was **Enochdian** would easily reach that place where our entire long life is located in its proper places within the **ether** <note>the celestial air or the upper atmosphere, thought to be the home of the stars and spirits</note>, and

<meta>catchword: stars (original: "sidq", likely for "sideraque")</meta>

### Chapter I.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'S' containing two human figures, one seated and one standing, intertwined with the letter's floral flourishes.</image-desc> Earlier, in the first book, I laid the foundations of long life; these we shall complete in this place. For I see no reason why I should repeat here those things which pertain to a whole and healthy life, since enough has been said about these by us in the previous books, specifically the second and third. Therefore, in this fifth book, I shall put the final touch <note>original: "supremam manum imponam"; a Latin idiom meaning to complete or perfect a work</note> to those things which we began in the first book regarding the life that exists beyond nature; and this according to theoretical reasoning.

First, we propose to all **spagyrists** <term>spagyricis: practitioners of "spagyric" or alchemical medicine, which involves the separation and purification of natural substances to create potent remedies</term> the age of both Adam and Methuselah <note>Biblical patriarchs renowned for their longevity; Adam is said to have lived 930 years and Methuselah 969 years</note>, as will be indicated in the second chapter that follows. In this, I advise that many things must be understood concerning **free will**,

### Chapter II.

But to finally bring to a conclusion those things which we began to discuss concerning long life, we will first indicate what "long life" is. The term of a long life encompasses within itself 1,000, or 900, or at the very least 600 years. Concerning the source of this life, which is beyond nature, understand it thus. There are two powers within the capacity of man: one is natural, of which we have spoken up to the point of one's innate end. The other is **aerial** <note>original: "aeria"; in Paracelsian thought, this refers to a spiritual or ethereal substance that is not bound by the weight and decay of the physical body</note>, for in this there is nothing corporeal. Therefore, incorporeal things complete this little book of ours.

Pity the mortals in this regard, to whom nature has refused the first and best treasure (which the monarchy of nature keeps locked within itself)—namely, the **light of nature** <term>lumen naturae: the innate wisdom and divine understanding present in the natural world and human reason, allowing one to perceive the "invisible" workings of creation</term>. Lest we labor further in this in vain: In philosophy, however, because the nature of the **aniadum** <term>aniadum: a Paracelsian term referring to the spiritual or celestial life-force that grants extraordinary longevity and vitality beyond the physical body's limits</term> defines and passes away, we shall remember it, and henceforth we shall make no mention of philoso-

...so that we may prove those things which we have begun to treat, and that not by philosophical reasoning, but by the **essence** <note>original: "essentia"; here referring to the fundamental spiritual substance of a thing</note> itself, which alone in this matter we have mentioned will have the greatest power. As for what pertains to the location and the changes of these things, we change nothing at all. Furthermore, what is the point of dying in vain for those things which the **light of nature** <term>light of nature: (Latin: "lumen naturae") the innate wisdom or divine intuition present in the natural world that allows humans to understand the mysteries of creation</term> has refused us? ### Chapter V.

I would not value even a hair the man who, under the guise of the **Lullian arts** <note>original: "Lullianis artibus"; referring to the complex logical, mystical, and alchemical systems of Ramon Llull (c. 1232–1315)</note>, falsely presents himself as a **necrolic practitioner** <note>original: "necroliū"</note>, and who rejects that which is contained within the four **Scaiolae** <term>Scaiolae: a Paracelsian term for specific spiritual or elemental forces that influence human health and the duration of life</term>, even while he proclaims me to be the supreme **Scaiola**. He does this so that, by the reasoning of the **cedurini** <note>original: "cedurinorum"; likely referring to a class of elemental spirits or alchemical entities within Paracelsus's cosmology</note>, I might be able to attack the necrolic arts. But I envy even the water-snakes for the Scaiolae, along with my rivals. What shall I say in this place of those things which **Musafagax** <note>a Paracelsian term likely personifying a specific imaginative power or a vital spirit governing certain bodily functions</note> includes in his rules, together with the narcotics of the four Scaiolae? These things fall asleep with you and render the temples of your head **anodyne** <term>anodyne: (from Greek "anodynos") a substance or state that relieves pain or induces a numb, painless calm</term>. 

And I lead you again and again into such a stupor that you are brought into the acquaintance of the **poppy** <note>original: "papaueris"; referring to opium and its sleep-inducing properties</note>. But I set those things aside and labor only in this: that which I have moved in the previous books—namely, in the **cosmographical life** <note>a life understood through its relationship to the macrocosm or the greater world-structure</note>, where the place and the body of the **Iesahach** <term>Iesahach: in Paracelsian thought, a term for the "spiritual" or "resurrected" body—an indestructible essence that transcends the mortal, elemental frame</term>...

Furthermore, those things which I prescribe—beyond the natural powers of a specific place or the physical body—I prescribe according to the **spagyric** <note>original: "spagyricæ"; refers to the alchemical art of separating and recombining substances to create potent medicines</note>, **hydromantic** <note>original: "hydromanticæ"; here referring to the medicinal or divinatory use of water and fluids</note>, and **pyrotechnic** <note>original: "pyrotenchans"; referring to the "arts of fire" or alchemical heating processes</note> arts.

Finally, let him labor to learn this fifth chapter, in which the **Scaiolae** <term>Scaiolae: spiritual or "mental stars" within the human mind that influence health and destiny</term> offend me the most. For in these, there is no mortality. 

Under this conclusion, therefore, I summarize the whole matter: I affirm that whoever lives according to the principle of the Scaiolae is immortal, a fact which I prove by the **Enochdians** <note>original: "enochdianos"; beings or principles associated with the biblical Enoch, who was said to have been "translated" by God to avoid death</note> and their followers. 

Nor shall the **aquaster** <term>aquaster: an astral or spiritual entity associated with the element of water and the "shadow" of the firmament</term> infringe upon this place. Furthermore, if I were to be inserted into the Scaiolae according to the reasoning of the Necrolics, it would be something to which I would take exception; however, that greatest **Adech** <term>Adech: the "Inner Man" or the archetypal spiritual force that governs human vital functions and formation</term> has anticipated this. He leads our purpose, though he does not dictate the manner of it: a matter which I leave to you, the Theorists, to discuss.

...transformation <note>original: "mutationem"; completing the word "transmutationem" from the previous page's catchword</note> **Melosine** <note>original: "Melosyne"; in Paracelsian thought, an elemental or imaginative force related to the soul's power to change</note> would have remained, if that difficult **Adech** <term>Adech: the internal "architect" or spiritual archetype of the human body that directs its growth and health</term> had consented—he who exists as both the death and the life of the **Scaiolae** <term>Scaiolae: the spiritual or "mental stars" within the human mind that influence health and destiny</term>. But in order for those things to be fixed, the deeds of Melosine refuse them; what kind of deeds they are, we shall set aside.

In order that this nature might be conceived in our minds, and so that we might reach the immortal year of the **aniadus** <note>original: "aniadin"</note>, we take up the characters of **Venus** <note>In alchemy, Venus often relates to the copper principle, the color green, or the generative power of nature</note>. Although you know these along with others, you do not put them into practice at all. We have fulfilled this very task by what we indicated in the earlier chapters, so that we may at last safely attain this life, in which the **aniadus** <term>Aniadus: the vital spiritual principle or "inner heaven" that governs longevity and resists decay</term> dominates and reigns, and remains with Him whom we serve without end. These and other secrets require nothing further. And in this way, we leave the work on long life concluded.

->### THEOPHRASTUS ON LIFE ###<-

...become apparent. Furthermore, the things I prescribe are beyond the powers of mere location and the physical body; I prescribe them according to the **spagyric** <term>spagyric: the alchemical art of separating components and recombining them to create potent medicines</term> arts, as well as the **hydromantic** <note>original: "hydromanticæ"; traditionally divination by water, here referring to a specific alchemical working with the watery element</note> and **pyrotechnic** <note>original: "pyrotenchans"; relating to the use of fire in chemical and spiritual operations</note> arts. 

But what does it profit me to mention these things to all of you, whose very names I do not know? For even if you act as **Necrolii** <term>Necrolii: in Paracelsian thought, these are spirits or forces that manage the transition between life and death, or the "dead" aspect of the inner body</term> and are somewhat experienced in the **cyphantus** <term>cyphantus: the "vessel of the mind" or the imaginative faculty that acts as a crucible for spiritual transformation</term>, your **vital pulses** <note>original: "pulsatiles"; the rhythmic life-forces or physical pulses of the body</note> nevertheless fall asleep. 

I have completed all these matters. Whoever understands them may, with the best right, sell himself under the name of a **spagyrist**. Then, let him labor to learn the fifth chapter, in which the **Scaiolae** <term>Scaiolae: spiritual "stars" or mental forces within the human mind that influence health, destiny, and the duration of life</term> most offend me. For in these there is no mortality. 

Regarding these things, therefore, I conclude the sum of the whole matter: I affirm that whoever lives according to the principle of the **Scaiolae** is immortal. This I prove through the **Enochdians** <note>original: "enochdianos"; a reference to those who, like the biblical Enoch, achieved a state of longevity or translation to a higher life</note> and their followers. Nor will the **aquaster** <term>aquaster: a visionary or "astral-watery" spirit that can mislead or influence the imagination</term> infringe upon this point. 

Furthermore, if I were to be inserted into the **Scaiolae** according to the principle of the **Necrolia** <note>original: "Necroliorum"; the terminal or death-related spiritual forces</note>, it would be something to which I would take exception. This is what the great **Adech** <term>Adech: the internal "architect" or spiritual archetype that directs the growth and preservation of the human form</term> prevents; he leads our purpose, though he does not dictate our method. 

I leave this for you, the **Theorists**, to discuss. And in this manner, the nature departed from its **nymph-like** <note>original: "nymphididica"; referring to the elemental nature of nymphs or water-spirits</note> state, through the intervention of the **Scaiolae**, into another trans- <note>The word is completed on the following page as "transmutation."</note>

<summary>Paracelsus asserts that immortality is possible by living according to the "Scaiolae" (mental-spiritual forces) and argues that the internal architect (Adech) governs the transformation of human nature.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, Spagyric, Scaiolae, Adech, Immortality, Enochdians, Cyphantus, Necrolia, Alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 213 ---
<meta>This page concludes the fifth book of "Of Long Life." It follows the discussion of the "Scaiolae" (internal spiritual forces) and their role in human mortality, transitioning from the previous page's discussion of the "whole trans-mutation" of the human state.</meta>

<page-num>95</page-num>
<header>LONG LIFE, BOOK V.</header>

...mutation Melusine <note>original: "Melofyne"; a reference to the folkloric elemental spirit Melusina, used here by Paracelsus to represent a specific state of alchemical transformation or the soul’s shifting nature</note> would remain, if that difficult **Adech** <term>Adech: the "inner architect" or spiritual archetype in the human body that governs the formation of life and health</term> had agreed, who is both the death and the life of the **Scaiolae** <term>Scaiolae: spiritual "stars" or mental forces within the human mind that influence health and destiny</term>. He agrees, moreover, to the first stages of time, but toward the end, he changes himself. From these things, I conclude that the celestial <note>original: "supermonica"; referring to heavenly or higher spiritual influences beyond the physical realm</note> figments open a window in the **cyphantis** <term>cyphantis: the "vessel of the mind" or imaginative faculty where spiritual transformations occur</term>. But so that these things might be fixed and solidified, they reject the deeds of Melusine; what those are like, we shall let pass. 

But let us turn to the **nymphididic** <term>nymphididic: pertaining to the elemental nature of nymphs or the life-force found in the "watery" or fluidic spiritual essence of man</term> nature. So that this nature may be conceived in our minds, and thus we may arrive at the immortal year of the **Aniadin** <term>Aniadin: the eternal life-force or the spiritual state of longevity that transcends physical decay</term>, we seize the characters of Venus <note>original: "characteres Veneris"; alchemical or astrological symbols representing harmony, attraction, and the generative power of the soul</note>, which even if you recognize them alongside others, you nevertheless use very little. We have fulfilled this very thing by what we indicated in the previous chapters, so that we may finally and securely attain this life, in which the **Aniadus** <term>Aniadus: the inward spiritual life-force that rules over the health and duration of the body</term> dominates and reigns, and remains with Him, whom we serve without end. These and other secrets require nothing more at all; and in this manner, we leave the work on long life concluded.

->The end of the five<-
->books on long life by<-
->Theophrastus of Hohenheim.<-

<sig>k 3</sig>

<summary>Paracelsus concludes his treatise on longevity by explaining that true long life is achieved through the alignment of the internal "architect" (Adech) and the spiritual life-force (Aniadus). He emphasizes that the mind's imaginative faculty must be opened to celestial influences to transcend mortality.</summary>
<keywords>Adech, Aniadus, Melusine, Scaiolae, Cyphantis, Venus, longevity, spiritual alchemy, immortality, Theophrastus von Hohenheim</keywords>

--- Page 214 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the conclusion of Book V of Paracelsus's "Of Long Life" to a new prefatory address by his student and editor, Adam von Bodenstein. Bodenstein's preface serves to defend his teacher's reputation and his specific medicinal preparations against contemporary critics.</meta>

# GREETINGS TO THE PIOUS READER FROM ADAM VON BODENSTEIN,
## Doctor of Philosophy and Medicine.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'C' with floral scrollwork patterns.</image-desc> Honest reader, I believe it escapes no one how poorly that divine philosopher and most celebrated of physicians, Aurelius Theophrastus Paracelsus, has been spoken of until now—not only among foreigners but even among his own people, namely the Germans. This is due to the perverse judgment of the unskilled, who claim he was the sort of man who wrote much but taught nothing of sound value, and who offered many medicines to the public that brought more harm than benefit to mankind. Among the number of these medicines, his **laudanum** <term>laudanum: in Paracelsus’s time, this referred to a "praiseworthy" alchemical remedy, often a complex pill containing opium, gold, and pearls, rather than the simple tincture of opium known today</term> has been unfavorably defamed by many until now as a ruinous and plainly fatal medicament. Therefore, to free Theophrastus himself and his laudanum—which is most worthy of the highest praise—from this calumny <note>calumny: a false and malicious statement intended to damage someone's reputation; slander</note>, I wish for all those of sound judgment to see and understand that it was produced from the true sources of the **arcana** <term>arcana: the "secret" or "hidden" essential powers of nature that a physician must learn to extract for healing</term> of nature and medicine...

<summary>This page contains a preface by Adam von Bodenstein defending Paracelsus against critics who accused his writings of being unsound and his medicines—specifically his "laudanum"—of being dangerous.</summary>
<keywords>Adam von Bodenstein, Paracelsus, laudanum, medical defense, arcana, German medicine, alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 215 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface by Adam von Bodenstein, transitioning from his defense of Paracelsus’s reputation to the specific inclusion of the recipe for "Laudanum." He frames the publication of this secret formula as a test of the reader’s gratitude.</meta>

To these books of **Theophrastus** <note>original: *Theophrasti*; Bodenstein refers to Paracelsus by his first name, Theophrastus</note> concerning **Long Life**, I wished to attach his own formula, so that it might be beneficial to good physicians, but for the foolish, a source of shame and a warning, so that in the future they might cease to condemn what they do not understand. I would indeed have added a most certain and supreme remedy against all kinds of poisons—not only those of plants, but also of animals, minerals, metals, and the **microcosm** <term>microcosm: a core concept in Paracelsian medicine where the human body is viewed as a "little world" that reflects the greater universe (macrocosm)</term>—but I have withheld it for this reason: to see how grateful you will show yourself to be toward our Theophrastus.

->The description of **Laudanum**, which he used in<-
->desperate and terminal diseases, is as<-
->follows:<-

Take one ounce of **foliated gold** <note>original: *orizei foliati*; likely a variation of *aurum foliatum* or gold leaf, used in alchemy for its supposed life-prolonging properties</note>,
   half an ounce of **unperforated pearls**,
   one dram each of **flowers of antimony** <term>flowers of antimony: a white powder (antimony trioxide) produced by heating antimony; it was a controversial and potent chemical remedy</term>
   and **asphalt** <note>original: *asphalti*; in this period, this often referred to bitumen or "mummy," a resinous substance used in healing</note>,
   three drams of **Oriental saffron**,
   **Roman myrrh**,
   and **Socotrine aloes** <note>aloes from the island of Socotra were considered the highest medical grade</note>, of each <term>ana: a medical shorthand meaning "equal parts of each"</term> up to the total weight of all the other ingredients; reduce these into a proper form.
The dose is from four grains, or seven, up to ten.

<summary>Bodenstein explains that he is including the recipe for Paracelsus’s famous Laudanum to silence critics and aid skilled doctors. The page provides the specific ingredients for this complex alchemical pill, which includes gold, pearls, and antimony.</summary>
<keywords>Laudanum recipe, Adam von Bodenstein, alchemical medicine, gold leaf, antimony, microcosm, medical ethics</keywords>

--- Page 216 ---
<meta>This page continues Adam von Bodenstein’s presentation of Paracelsian recipes, following the first "Laudanum" formula described on page 97. It provides a second, specialized recipe specifically for treating fevers.</meta>

### There is also another most excellent **Laudanum** <term>Laudanum: in this era, the term did not yet refer specifically to opium tincture, but to any "secret" or "praiseworthy" compound remedy developed by Paracelsus</term> against fevers, which contains:

->One pound of **prepared antimony**,<-
->one medicinal pound of **white sugar**,<-

Let a fine powder be made; let it be distilled in sand or a **water bath** <term>balneo: short for *balneum Mariae* or "Mary's bath," a double-boiler used for gentle, even heating</term> according to the art. Of this oil, take two ounces,

->one ounce of **Socotrine aloes**,<-
->half an ounce of **ambergris** <note>original: *ambræ*; likely ambergris, a rare substance derived from whales, though it could occasionally refer to fossilized resin (amber)</note>,<-
->six drams of the most choice **saffron**,<-

Let them be reduced successively into a mass; let small pills be made; let three be given with **conserve of borage** <note>borage was a common herb in the Renaissance pharmacy, believed to "expel melancholy" and cool the blood during fevers</note> before the onset of the fever, and let a **sweat** <note>original: *sudor*; the induction of heavy perspiration was a standard therapeutic goal to "purge" the body of the morbid matter causing the fever</note> be induced, if possible.

->Rejoice in these things, and enjoy them to the praise<-
->of **Jesus Christ**, and<-
->farewell.<-

<meta>Small decorative scroll ornament at bottom right. Faint bleed-through of text from reverse side is visible.</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the section with a second recipe for "Laudanum" specifically formulated for fevers, utilizing distilled antimony, sugar, and botanical ingredients. It ends with a traditional pious closing, attributing the medical knowledge to the praise of Christ.</summary>
<keywords>antimony, Laudanum, fever remedies, distillation, Socotrine aloes, saffron, borage, Renaissance pharmacology, Adam von Bodenstein</keywords>
